# Storm Bear — Claude Context File

This vault is a personal knowledge base and operating system for Storm Bear, built on the LLM Wiki pattern.


## Who I Am & My Purpose

I'm a software developer and Scrum Coach. I build software and help teams ship.

My purpose is building a persistent, compounding knowledge base following Karpathy's LLM Wiki pattern. The idea: instead of re-deriving knowledge from scratch on every query, the LLM incrementally builds and maintains a structured wiki — summaries, entity pages, cross-references, evolving synthesis. I curate sources, direct analysis, and ask the right questions. The LLM handles all the grunt work. Obsidian is the IDE, the LLM is the programmer, the wiki is the codebase.


## Claude's Purpose in This Level

At the vault level, Claude is primarily the wiki maintainer and knowledge architect:

- **Ingest sources** — read new sources, extract key information, write summary pages, update the index, update entity/concept pages across the wiki, append to the log
- **Maintain cross-references** — keep links between pages current, flag contradictions, note where new data challenges old claims
- **Answer queries** — search the wiki, synthesize answers with citations, and file valuable answers back into the wiki as new pages
- **Lint the wiki** — periodically check for contradictions, stale claims, orphan pages, missing cross-references, data gaps
- **Strategic thinking** — help with planning, accountability, brainstorming, and decision-making when called on

The prime directive: don't repeat the same mistake twice.


## Claude's Rules & Boundaries

- **Blunt and direct** — challenge me, don't sugarcoat, call me out when I'm wrong
- **Always end with a suggested next action** — every response should close with what to do next
- **Prefix AI-generated files with `(C)`** — so it's clear what Claude wrote vs. what I wrote
- **Ask before editing existing notes** — never modify my files without permission
- **NEVER fabricate information** — if you don't know, say so. No making things up.
- **NEVER make silent assumptions** — if something is unclear, ask. Don't fill in the blanks and hope I won't notice.


## Vault State Architecture (refactored 2026-04-27 session 67)

**Why this exists:** Pre-refactor CLAUDE.md was 584KB / ~146K tokens — too large for any agent to read without context-thrashing. Three consecutive v56 wiki-build attempts failed at the 4-tool-use mark because agents couldn't fit even one vault file in context. This validated routine v2.1 operator-facing-backlog discipline (35 sessions deferred = 7× threshold) empirically as an INFRASTRUCTURE BLOCK, not a soft warning.

**Refactor:** Bulk state moved to `_state/` chapter files. Each chapter sized <35K tokens so agents can read individual chapters without thrashing. **Reading discipline for agents:**
- Never read more than 1 chapter file in a single session — context-thrashing risk
- Use `awk` via Bash for surgical line-range extracts when full-chapter not needed
- Pattern Library specific entries: read `PATTERN_LIBRARY.md` chapter files (also refactored, see below)
- For wiki builds: read routine + 1 most-recent project chapter (03c) + WebFetch new subject — should fit in ~50-80K tokens

**Authoritative state pointer:** Chapter files are the authoritative state record. Root CLAUDE.md (this file) is the entry-point shim only. Updates go to chapter files; root only updated when adding new chapters or changing this index.

Two on-goal bridges keep it above (a)-FAIL→OFF-GOAL: **(1)** the repo deliberately ships a **first-party Claude Code Agent Skill** (`AGENTS.md` "Agent Entry Point" + `timesfm-forecasting/SKILL.md`, community-contributor **Clayton Young @borealBytes**; agentskills.io standard — a well-built skill with a mandatory preflight system-checker + dataset-fit memory estimation + quantile-based anomaly detection + a backtest recipe) + a Vertex "dockerized endpoint for agentic calling" → packaged to be agent-native, on the exact agent-skills substrate the vault studies; **(2)** the realistic USE is **Claude-Code-ORCHESTRATED** (schema→query→forecast→backtest→gate→PR = a genuine autonomous-agent-for-software task). Plus a sharp **Goal-#2 (hireui) payoff** (recruitment forecasting). ⭐ **Sharp cross-ref to v192 palmier-pro:** the "product/model retrofitted to be agent-native via a first-party agent interface" meta-move — palmier-pro=MCP-on-a-video-editor, TimesFM=Agent-Skill+Vertex-endpoint-on-a-forecasting-model; 2 instances → recorded DEFERRED watch axis, NOT minted at N=2 across such different shapes. **ARCHITECTURE (2.5, source-verified from `src/timesfm/`):** 200M decoder transformer; 20 layers · model_dims 1280 · 16 heads (head-dim 80); RoPE + RMSNorm (pre-LN, QK-norm, fused QKV, per-dim scale); SiLU/swish FF; input patch 32 / output patch 128 (larger output → fewer AR steps); context_limit 16,384; 10 output slices (deciles q10–q90 + mean; idx0=mean, 1=q10…9=q90); ReVIN reversible instance-norm + Welford running-stats; KV-cache autoregressive decode; scale/shift-equivariance `TimesFM(aX+b)=a·TimesFM(X)+b`; XReg covariates via `forecast_with_covariates()` (in-context linear reg). **API:** `timesfm.TimesFM_2p5_200M_torch.from_pretrained("google/timesfm-2.5-200m-pytorch")` → `.compile(ForecastConfig(max_context, max_horizon, normalize_inputs, use_continuous_quantile_head, infer_is_positive, fix_quantile_crossing, …))` → `.forecast(horizon, inputs=[np arrays])` → (point (N,H), quantiles (N,H,10)). **PRETRAINING (paper-stated):** ~100B time-points (Wikipedia pageviews + Google Trends + synthetic ARMA/seasonal/trend/step), ~80% real / 20% synthetic; 16 TPUv5e ~2 days; MSE + random patch masking + RIN. **BENCHMARKS (paper-stated):** Monash geomean scaled-MAE 0.6846 vs supervised N-BEATS 0.7005; ETT ~0.36 ≈ supervised PatchTST 0.37 (zero-shot ≈ supervised, 200M vs 175B GPT-3.5-based). **Landscape (leaderboard-stated, churns):** TimesFM 2.5 led GIFT-Eval on release (Sept 2025) but is now a strong **#2** — **Chronos-2 (Amazon, Oct 2025) leads** (~79.8% vs ~70.0% win-rate, competitor-paper-stated); Moirai-2.0 (Salesforce) native-multivariate #3; IBM TTM tiny/CPU; Toto (Datadog) observability; TimeGPT (Nixtla) closed/API; Moment (CMU) multi-task. TimesFM wins longest context (16k) + param-efficiency (200M) + Google Cloud integration; loses multivariate (→Moirai-2.0)/pure-accuracy (→Chronos-2)/edge-CPU (→IBM TTM). **DEPLOYMENT:** PyPI `pip install timesfm[torch|flax|xreg]`; **★ PRIOR HEAD — v186 DeepSpec ship COMPLETE 2026-07-01 — DeepSeek's speculative-DECODING training framework (⚠️ NOT spec-driven-development — the name misleads: `Spec`=Speculative decoding); competitor model/inference-substrate subject; GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4 PER OPERATOR DIRECTION [(a) FAIL · (b) MODERATE · (c) STRONG · (d) STRONG] (OFF-GOAL CAPTURE equally/more defensible — the GLM-5 v176 template, arguably MORE off-goal); NO MINT (corpus-knowledge data-point; counts 46/11 UNCHANGED, §C surface unchanged); operator-requested ("build LLM wiki + double deep dive + show many pilot methods").** `deepseek-ai/DeepSpec` (MIT; Python 97.9%; no releases; 5.4k★/428 forks page-stated; cloned + source-verified at commit **`afdfa7c`**, last commit 2026-06-30). README line 3: *"a full-stack codebase for training and evaluating draft models for speculative decoding."* Trains small **draft models** that make a big LLM generate tokens faster, **losslessly** (rejection sampling preserves the target distribution exactly). **3 drafter algorithms:** **DSpark** (DeepSeek's own + paper), **DFlash** (z-lab), **Eagle3** (baseline, adapted from SGLang's **SpecForge**). Targets Qwen3-{4B,8B,14B} + Gemma-4-12B; released HF checkpoints. Author **= DeepSeek-AI** (org `deepseek-ai`; 94.7k followers). **THE NOVEL METHOD (DSpark, "double deep dive"):** paper *"DSpark: Confidence-Scheduled Speculative Decoding with Semi-Autoregressive Generation"* (Peking Univ + DeepSeek-AI incl. founder **Wenfeng Liang**). (1) **Semi-autoregressive** = heavy **parallel backbone** (DFlash — KV-injection of target hidden states) + a **lightweight sequential head** (Markov `B=W1[x_{k-1}]·W2` low-rank r=256 / RNN) killing "suffix decay." (2) **Confidence-scheduled verification** = a confidence head predicts per-position prefix-survival → a hardware-aware scheduler (Algorithm 1) spends target verification budget only where payoff is likely (Θ=τ·SPS(B)). Frozen target; draft shares embed+LM-head; 3 losses (CE + total-variation + confidence-BCE) at α=0.1/0.9/1.0. **Deployed in DeepSeek-V4 (Flash+Pro previews): +60–85% / +57–78% per-user gen speed vs the prior MTP-1 baseline** (DeepSeek's own unreplicated numbers). ⚠️ **Code-vs-paper scope gap (documented):** the **production Hardware-Aware Prefix Scheduler (Algorithm 1) is NOT in the open repo** — the eval code has only a static confidence threshold + offline calibration; the open repo = training + offline-eval framework. **(a) FAILS cleanly** (Chinese frontier-model lab, not Anthropic; declared-non-Anthropic-institution [ByteDance v143 / NVIDIA v169 / Zhipu-GLM-5 v176 / Microsoft-SkillOpt v178]; ⚠️ plausibly **first *first-party* deepseek-ai org subject** — DeepSeek-TUI v72 was third-party [`Hmbown`], GLM-5 = Zhipu → #19 19a). **(b) MODERATE keys the tier (operator-directed; ⚠️ (b) FAIL → OFF-GOAL equally/more defensible)** — spec-decoding is the engine layer under agent inference cost/latency + lands on the `claude-api-cost-optimization` thread; **held below STRONG** by: a *training framework for draft models* (8 GPU + 38 TB, nothing to install-and-use), targets **open models NOT Claude** (closed-weight → cannot swap a draft model in; **MORE off-goal than GLM-5**, which was at least a routable Claude-Code backend), substrate is tools-around-Claude not inference-accel research. **(c) STRONG** (novel published method deployed in DeepSeek-V4 + full FSDP training/eval framework; caveats: novelty in the *method/paper*, repo forks SpecForge + builds on DFlash, production scheduler absent from repo). **(d) STRONG** (model/inference-substrate cluster GLM-5 v176 / DeepSeek-TUI v72 / cc-switch v73 / LLMs-from-scratch v74 / fish-speech v20 + cost-optimization thread). **Pattern outcome: NO MINT** — no new top-level pattern (max #85), no new §C standalone, **counts UNCHANGED 46/11**, §C surface unchanged. Corpus-FIRST for the *speculative-decoding / inference-acceleration domain* within the vault (hand-grep: no prior spec-decoding / draft-model / EAGLE / DFlash / MTP subject) → a mint WOULD be corpus-first as a class, **DECLINED + recorded as a DEFERRED watch axis** (the GLM-5 v176 / cortex-hub v181 template): goal-adjacent single subject (§28 phantom-count inflation) + **2nd data-point in the DEFERRED "model/inference-substrate" tier** (GLM-5 = frontier model @N=1; DeepSpec = inference-accel framework — different sub-class → strengthens the tier @N=2, doesn't cross the mint bar) + Kilo-Code-v177 "mint the exemplar" precedent is for in-scope agent-capability classes. **SECONDARY (NOT minted):** #19 19a first first-party deepseek-ai · **NOT #43** (LlamaFactory v22 / Unsloth = weight-space *training* optimizers; **★ EARLIER — v180 a.i-assistant-chatbot-telegram-serverless ship COMPLETE 2026-06-22 — a self-hosted, Telegram-fronted, serverless-on-your-own-PC personal agentic framework; GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4 [(a) FAIL · (b) MODERATE · (c) MODERATE · (d) STRONG]; NO MINT (corpus-knowledge data-point; counts 46/10 UNCHANGED, §C surface ≈36 unchanged); a goal-relevant agent-framework class at (b) MODERATE, Gemini-bound.** `badpaybad/a.i-assistant-chatbot-telegram-serverles` (repo slug spelled "serverles", missing a final **s**; MIT; 6★/2 forks) = README headline (VI) *"Biến PC của bạn thành 1 AI chat bot assistant (PC A.I Agentic framework)… chạy hoàn toàn tự động tại private PC của bạn, không cần server, không cần domain, không cần database server, bảo mật, an toàn dữ liệu, 24/24."* You command a personal agent **entirely through a Telegram chat**: Telegram → webhook on your PC (exposed via a **free Cloudflare Tunnel** subdomain, re-registered every startup) → **FastAPI** (`program.py`, :8088) → an **LLM orchestration layer** (`knowledgebase/orchestrationcontext.py` — builds context from summaries + recent messages + current message + URLs + scraped content + files, then `skills_decision()` picks/combines skills) → cli/browser/jira/googledrive/filesfolders skills → reply in the same chat. **LLM = Google Gemini by default** (`gemini-2.0-flash`); a bundled **local Gemma-4 server** (`gemma4/program.py`) re-implements the **Gemini `v1beta generateContent` API** so the same client runs fully local (README: AMD iGPU 780M / ROCm). **Long-term memory** = a **FAISS** `LocalVectorDB` (gemini-embedding-001 3072-d / text-embedding-004 / **local fasttext** 300-d); short-term = rolling chat summarization; storage = shared **SQLite** with JSON query. **★ EARLIER — v178 SkillOpt ship COMPLETE 2026-06-22 — CORPUS-FIRST text-space skill optimizer (training-loop optimization of a frozen agent's natural-language skill document via validation-gated edits); GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4 [(a) FAIL · (b) STRONG · (c) STRONG · (d) STRONG]; 1 NEW §C standalone (N=1); counts 46/10 UNCHANGED; on the goal-#1 agent-skills substrate core.** `microsoft/SkillOpt` = *"a text-space optimizer that trains reusable natural-language skills for frozen LLM agents through trajectory-driven edits, validation-gated updates, and deployable best_skill.md artifacts"* (repo desc); README hook *"Train agent skills like you train neural networks — with epochs, (mini-)batchsize, learning rates, and validation gates — but without touching model weights."* It treats **the skill document as the trainable state of a frozen agent**: a *separate optimizer model* turns scored rollouts into **bounded add/delete/replace** edits, and **a candidate edit is accepted only when it strictly improves a held-out validation score** (loop = rollout→reflect→edit→validate; textual learning-rate budget; rejected-edit buffer; epoch-wise meta-update; deployable `best_skill.md` ≈300–2,000 tok; zero inference-time overhead). Backed by a paper (Yang et al. 2026, **arXiv 2605.23904**, "SkillOpt: Executive Strategy for Self-Evolving Agent Skills"; MS Research page + HF papers). **Target models (7):** OpenAI/Azure/**Claude**/Qwen/MiniMax/**GPT-5.5**/Codex; **harnesses (3):** direct chat / **Codex CLI** / **Claude Code CLI** (+19.1-pt lift inside Claude Code); 6 benchmarks; WebUI. **Python 86.6% / HTML 12.2% / Shell 1.2%; MIT; v0.1.0 (Jun 2 2026); 1 release; PyPI `pip install skillopt`; 8.6k★/828 forks (page-stated).** Author **= Microsoft** (a Microsoft Research project). **GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4.** **(a) FAILS cleanly** — Microsoft = major US corporation/research lab, not Anthropic ((a)-7 Anthropic-only); declared-non-Anthropic-institution (Google v140 / ByteDance v143 / NVIDIA v169 / OpenBMB v175 / Kilo AI v177); no heritage rescue. ⚠️ **NOT corpus-first Microsoft** (collision-grep-verified — **markitdown v28** `microsoft/markitdown` + **spec-kit v17** `github/spec-kit` precede it; SkillOpt is plausibly the first Microsoft *Research* / *agent-skills* subject, a thin sub-distinction → a **#19 19a returning-institution data-point**, an over-claim caught + corrected via grep per `feedback_wiki_verify_independently_check_collisions`). **(b) STRONG (keys the tier)** — it optimizes **agent skills** = the *exact* artifact this vault produces (`05 Skills/` SKILL.md), dead-center on the Goal #1 agent-skills substrate; runs on Claude + Claude Code CLI; a methodology+tool directly pilotable into the vault's own skills. **★ EARLIER — v173 claude-tap ship COMPLETE 2026-06-20 — CORPUS-FIRST wire-level API-traffic interceptor / trace-viewer (protocol-inspection proxy) for coding agents; stays on the goal-#1 substrate core (3rd consecutive core-substrate ship).** `liaohch3/claude-tap` = *"a local proxy and trace viewer for AI coding agents"* — you run your coding-agent CLI **through** claude-tap; it sits in the middle as a **man-in-the-middle proxy**, forwards traffic unchanged, **records every request/response pair** (*"system prompts, conversation history, tool schemas, tool calls, streaming responses, token usage, and request diffs"*), and renders them in a browser trace-viewer for **debugging by evidence**. Viewer: **structural diff** between consecutive requests (char-level highlighting) · full-text search · path filter · token-usage breakdown (in/out/cache) · tool-schema inspector · self-contained HTML / JSONL export · live viewer · dark/light · iframe embed · i18n (8 langs). **Dual reverse/forward proxy modes** tap **14 client CLIs** (claude/agy/codex/codexapp/gemini/kimi/kimi-code/opencode/openclaw/pi/hermes/cursor/qoder/codebuddy); low-overhead SSE/WebSocket relay; **100% local** (*"no hosted dashboard is required, and common auth headers are redacted before recording"*). Install `uv tool install claude-tap` / `pip install claude-tap` (Python 3.11+). **Python 82.4% / JS 9.7% / HTML 4.5% / CSS 2.6%; MIT; v0.1.120 (2026-06-18); 100+ releases; 9 contributors.** Related: **Phistory** (`WEIFENG2333/phistory`) archives system-prompt snapshots using claude-tap's *"capture-only prompt export"* (downstream dependent). > **Prior — v178 SkillOpt (2026-06-22, GOAL-ALIGNED INCLUDE 3/4 — CORPUS-FIRST text-space skill optimizer; on the goal-#1 agent-skills substrate core):** `microsoft/SkillOpt` = *"a text-space optimizer that trains reusable natural-language skills for frozen LLM agents through trajectory-driven edits, validation-gated updates, and deployable best_skill.md artifacts"* (repo desc); README hook *"Train agent skills like you train neural networks — with epochs, (mini-)batchsize, learning rates, and validation gates — but without touching model weights."* It treats **the skill document as the trainable state of a frozen agent**: a separate optimizer model turns scored rollouts into **bounded add/delete/replace** edits, and **an edit is accepted only when it strictly improves a held-out validation score** (loop = rollout→reflect→edit→validate; textual learning-rate budget; rejected-edit buffer; epoch-wise meta-update; deployable `best_skill.md` ≈300–2,000 tok; zero inference-time overhead). Paper: Yang et al. 2026, **arXiv 2605.23904**, "Executive Strategy for Self-Evolving Agent Skills" (MS Research + HF papers). Target models (7, incl. **Claude**/GPT-5.5/Codex); harnesses (3: direct chat / **Codex CLI** / **Claude Code CLI**, +19.1-pt lift inside Claude Code); 6 benchmarks; WebUI. **Python 86.6% / HTML 12.2%; MIT; v0.1.0 (Jun 2 2026); 1 release; `pip install skillopt`; 8.6k★/828 forks (page-stated).** Author **= Microsoft** (Microsoft Research). **(a) FAIL** (Microsoft = declared-non-Anthropic-institution like Google v140 / NVIDIA v169 / OpenBMB v175 / Kilo AI v177; no heritage rescue; ⚠️ **NOT corpus-first Microsoft** — markitdown v28 + spec-kit v17 `github/` precede it → #19 19a returning-institution data-point, over-claim caught + corrected via grep) · **(b) STRONG** (optimizes **agent skills** = the exact `05 Skills/` SKILL.md artifact the vault produces, dead-center on Goal #1; runs on Claude + Claude Code CLI; directly pilotable into the vault's own skills; STRONG-not-STRONGEST = MS Research third-party + GPT-5.5-headline multi-model — a clear tier above GLM-5 v176's (b) MODERATE) · **(c) STRONG** (real optimizer engine; 6 benchmarks × 7 models × 3 harnesses; WebUI; PyPI; arXiv paper; caveat: young v0.1.0/1 release) · **(d) STRONG.** **Pattern outcome: 1 NEW §C standalone (CORPUS-FIRST, N=1) "Text-Space Skill Optimizer — Training-Loop Optimization of a Frozen Agent's Natural-Language Skill Document via Validation-Gated Edits"** — the corpus's FIRST *optimizer* for agent skills.
